VERB: biegen
NOTE: In the sense of bending, the auxiliary verb is “haben” (e.g. “ich habe das Rohr gebogen”). In the sense of turning, which is used mostly with the prefix “ab-”, the auxiliary verb is “sein”, e.g. “ich bin rechts abgebogen”.
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>ich  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::biege::biegen}}</span></div>

VERB: dürfen
NOTE: The first person singular is “ichdarf”. For subjunctive, only “dürfte” is possible (“würde dürfen” does not exist). In the past, “durfte” is usually preferred. When used with another infinitive, past participle changes to the infinitive, e.g. “ich habe es machen dürfen”.
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>ich  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::darf::dürfen}}</span></div>

<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>Sie  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::dürfen::dürfen}}</span></div>

VERB: fahren
NOTE: In the sense of movement, the auxiliary verb is “sein”. Fahren can also mean “to drive a vehicle”, in which case the auxiliary verb is “haben”, e.g. “er hat den Wagen nach Berlin gefahren”.

VERB: fliegen

VERB: kommen

VERB: können
NOTE: The first person singular is “ich kann”. For subjunctive, only “könnte” is possible (“würde können” does not exist). In the past, “konnte” is usually preferred. When used with another infinitive, past participle changes to the infinitive, e.g. “ich habe es machen können”.

VERB: mögen

VERB: müssen
NOTE: The first person singular is “ich muss”. For subjunctive, only “müsste” is possible (“würde müssen” does not exist). In the past, “musste” is usually preferred. When used with another infinitive, past participle changes to the infinitive, e.g. “ich habe es machen müssen”.

VERB: reiten
NOTE: For most meanings both “haben” and “sein” as auxiliary verbs are possible, but for the meaning “to ride a horse”, “sein” is preferred (e.g. “er ist nach Berlin geritten”). If it is used to specify the ridden animal, the auxiliary verb is “haben”, e.g. “er hat ein Kamel geritten” (he rode a camel).

VERB: schwimmen
NOTE: When the verb signifies movement, the auxiliary verb is “sein”, e.g. “er ist zur Insel geschwommen” (he swam to the island). When it means just the activity, both “haben” and “sein” are possible, “haben” is preferred in the North of Germany, “sein” in the South.
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>ich  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwimme::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>du  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwimmst::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>er  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwimmt::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>wir  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwimmen::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>ihr  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwimmt::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>Sie  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwimmen::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="preterit-border">← Gestern, ←<br>ich  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwamm::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="preterit-border">← Gestern, ←<br>du  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwammst::schwimmen}}</span></div>

<div class="preterit-border">← Gestern, ←<br>wir  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwammen::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="preterit-border">← Gestern, ←<br>ihr  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwammt::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="preterit-border">← Gestern, ←<br>Sie  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwammen::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="subjunctive-border">↫ Wenn es möglich wäre, ↫<br>ich  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwömme::schwimmen}}</span></div>
<div class="subjunctive-border">↫ Wenn es möglich wäre, ↫<br>du  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwömmst::schwimmen}}</span></div>

<div class="subjunctive-border">↫ Wenn es möglich wäre, ↫<br>Sie  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::schwömmen::schwimmen}}</span></div>

VERB: senden
NOTE: This conjugation is used when “senden” means “to send something physically”, such as a package or a letter. It can also mean “to broadcast or send electronically”, but in this sense it is regular, e.g. “ich habe ihm eine E-Mail gesendet” (I have sent him an e-mail).
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>ich  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::sende::senden}}</span></div>

<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>wir  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::senden::senden}}</span></div>
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>ihr  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::sendet::senden}}</span></div>
<div class="present-border">⊙ Jetzt gerade, ⊙<br>Sie  <span class="de_verb">{{c1::senden::senden}}</span></div>

VERB: sollen
NOTE: The first person singular is “ich soll”. For subjunctive, only “sollte” is possible (“würde sollen” does not exist). In the past, “sollte” is usually preferred. When used with another infinitive, past participle changes to the infinitive, e.g. “ich habe es machen sollen”.

VERB: streichen

VERB: treiben
NOTE: The auxiliary verb is “haben” in all meanings apart from “being moved forward”, e.g. “das Eis ist auf dem Spiegel des Flusses getrieben” (the ice moved on the surface of the river).

VERB: treten
NOTE: In the meaning “to step somewhere”, the auxiliary verb is “haben”, e.g. “beim Tanzen hat er seiner Partnerin auf den Fuß getreten” (he stepped on his partner’s foot while dancing”. In the meaning “to make a few steps”, the auxiliary verb is “sein”, e.g. “er ist zu uns getreten” (he approached us).

VERB: ziehen
NOTE: The auxiliary verb “sein” is used in the sense “to wander, to move”, e.g. “er ist nach Berlin gezogen” (he moved to Berlin), and similarly for related “umziehen” (to move (the place where you live) completely).
